# RC Car Simulator Implementation Plan

> **For agentic workers:** REQUIRED SUB-SKILL: Use superpowers:subagent-driven-development (recommended) or superpowers:executing-plans to implement this plan task-by-task. Steps use checkbox (`- [ ]`) syntax for tracking.

**Goal:** A PWM-level RC car simulator (`src/sim/autosdv_rc_sim`) that runs the car's own vehicle interface and `AckermannSpeedPID` against a slip-capable rear-drive model up to 11 m/s, grades Lab 2 Part 1 submissions headlessly and deterministically, and runs the full Autoware planning-simulator stack on a CPU-only laptop.

**Architecture:** The vehicle interface's two hardware touchpoints (PCA9685, Jetson.GPIO) get simulated backends (`hardware:=real|sim`), and its logic moves into plain classes (`ActuatorCore`, `VelocityEstimator`) that the ROS nodes only wrap. A pure-Python plant (ESC, DC motor with battery sag, Karnopp wheels, Pacejka combined slip, a CommonRoad single-track drift chassis blended into the kinematic bicycle at low speed) is stepped at 1 kHz, either by a one-process lockstep harness for grading or by a ROS node that owns `/clock` for interactive runs. A track generator sizes one straight from the graded speed profile, and a replay node shows a graded trace in Autoware's RViz.

**Tech Stack:** Python 3.10 (Ubuntu 22.04), ROS 2 Humble, Autoware 1.5.0 Debians (`/opt/autoware/1.5.0`), rclpy, PyYAML, pytest, play_launch, just. No numpy on the lockstep path.

**Spec:** docs/design/rc-sim-design.md

## Global Constraints

- Autoware 1.5.0 from `/opt/autoware/1.5.0`, ROS 2 Humble, Ubuntu 22.04. Never patch `/opt/autoware`; every change lives in this repo or a submodule.
- The plant, harness, grader, track generator and replay are Python only (ament_python). The headless grader needs Python + PyYAML and no running ROS graph.
- Target machine: a CPU-only laptop with 4 cores and 8 GB, no NVIDIA driver. The full stack must use under 3 cores and 4 GB without RViz.
- Grading is deterministic: traces and marks are bit-identical whatever the CPU and its load. The lockstep path uses only IEEE basic arithmetic, `math.sqrt`, `math.floor`, `math.ldexp`, `math.copysign` and `round`. It never calls libm transcendentals, `**`, numpy or `random`, with one documented exception: `ActuatorCore.run_steering_control` (production code, moved verbatim from the actuator) calls `math.tan`/`math.atan`, and its result is truncated to an integer PWM. The straight track commands steering of about 0, and the golden-digest and CPU-stress tests (Task 17) are the net for a last-bit difference. The determinism lint names this exception in its allow-list.
- Physics runs at 1 kHz (`physics_dt` 0.001 s). The control tick is 10 ms (actuator `rate` 100 Hz). `velocity_report` publishes at 20 Hz (50 ms).
- Rear-wheel drive. The motor and the ESC brake act on the rear axle only, and the front wheels roll freely.
- Steering lock is 0.349 rad (`max_steer_angle`, vehicle_info.param.yaml). The simulator runs the actuator with `tire_angle_to_steer_ratio: -143.0`, as Lab 2 does, so that ±50 servo counts span ±0.349 rad.
- PWM counts from actuator.yaml: `min_pwm` 360, `init_pwm` 370, `max_pwm` 470, `brake_pwm` 340, steering 439/489/539, `pwm_freq` 60 Hz.
- Wheel radius is 0.0525 m (velocity_report.yaml `wheel_diameter` 10.5 cm). There are 12 hall markers per wheel revolution, on a rear wheel.
- The motor is a **mock**: K_v 3000 rpm/V, so K_t = K_e = 60/(2π·3000) = 0.003183 N·m/A. Winding plus ESC resistance is 0.03 Ω. Total gear ratio G is 14.5:1. The battery is 3S LiPo, 11.1 V nominal, 0.03 Ω internal.
- Track profile: 0 → 2 → 6 → 11 m/s, holds T = 3 s, a = 2.5 m/s², d = 2.5 m/s², run-out 10 m. The total is 115.4 m, the stop line is at 105.4 m, and the run takes about 17.8 s.
- Student-tunable keys, and nothing else: `kp_speed`, `ki_speed`, `kd_speed`, `integral_limit`, `enable_conditional_integration`, `derivative_filter_alpha`, `pwm_output_alpha`, `max_pwm_offset`. `max_pwm_offset` is new: default 55 (`(max_pwm − min_pwm) // 2`), at most `max_pwm − init_pwm` = 100.
- Production behaviour is unchanged when the new parameters are unset. Unset `max_pwm_offset` keeps 370 ± 55. Unset `hardware` means `real`. `use_sim_time:=false` keeps the wall-clock timing exactly as today.
- `hardware:=real` is the default on the car.
- ROS tests on this shared host run as `ROS_DOMAIN_ID=73 flock tmp/bench.lock …`, never on domain 0. Stacks are started with `setsid` and stopped by process group.
- Temporary files go to `./tmp/` (gitignored), never `/tmp`. That includes pytest's: the package's `test/conftest.py` sets `--basetemp` to `tmp/pytest/rc_sim` unless the caller gives one.
- Submodule lockstep: `src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch` tracks branch `1.5.0`. Check out `1.5.0` before editing (never commit on a detached HEAD). Commit and push the submodule first, then commit the new pin in the superproject and push `develop`.
- Shell: commands below are bash. The login shell is fish, so run each block under `bash -lc '…'` or in a bash terminal.
- Every commit message ends with the two trailer lines. Define them once per shell:

  ```bash
  TRAILER=$'Co-Authored-By: Claude Opus 5.5 <noreply@anthropic.com>\nClaude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Gc3avTBSVPQzUwu2gVHKc5'
  ```

  and commit with `git commit -m "<subject>" -m "$TRAILER"`. Stage only the paths each task names.
- `develop` takes direct pushes, with linear history only. Use `git pull --rebase origin develop` before pushing.

## Review Focus

Six failure modes the spec implies that a straightforward test suite would miss, most likely first. Each one has a pinning test in the task that owns it.

1. **Brake authority depends on the ESC's brake endpoint.** The shell clamps the controller to `[min_pwm, max_pwm]` = [360, 470], so the controller can brake only between 365 (the deadband edge) and 360. If the model put the ESC's full-brake endpoint anywhere below 360 (for example at 270, mirroring forward), the controller could not brake harder than about 1.3 m/s². No submission could then follow the profile's 2.5 m/s² to the stop line, and the braking scenario would grade the model instead of the student. The plan takes `min_pwm` 360 as the full-brake/full-reverse endpoint, as actuator.yaml documents it ("drives backward at max speed"), with `brake_torque_max` sized so that the rear tyre force, road load included, stays 10 % under the nominal rear grip. *Pinning tests:* `test_min_pwm_is_full_brake` (Task 7) and `test_brake_authority_and_overshoot` (Task 13), which shows that a mirrored endpoint would put the car more than 20 m past the stop line.
2. **The actuator times itself on the wall clock.** `actuator.py` uses `time.time()` for the PID's dt and `time.monotonic()` for its watchdogs. With `use_sim_time` and a plant running faster or slower than real time, the dt is wrong and the watchdogs fire spuriously. The node therefore takes its time from the ROS clock when `use_sim_time` is true, and keeps `time.time()`/`time.monotonic()` otherwise. *Pinning test:* `test_sim_time_drives_dt_and_watchdogs` (Task 4).
3. **Slip at v → 0, and a hall sensor on the driven wheel.** κ = (ωr − v)/max(|v|, v_min) is a ratio that explodes at standstill, so a launch-spin limit written on κ fails every launch. Separately, the sensor is on a *rear* (driven) wheel: in a spin it reports the wheel's speed rather than the car's, and in a lock it reports 0 while the car slides. Metrics use the slip *velocity* ωr − v for launches, κ only above 1 m/s, and always the true chassis speed. *Pinning tests:* `test_hall_follows_rear_wheel_during_spin` (Task 11) and `test_launch_slip_metric_finite_at_standstill` (Task 15).
4. **Bit-identity across CPUs.** glibc dispatches `sin`/`atan`/`exp`/`pow` to FMA or non-FMA variants per CPU, and numpy picks SIMD kernels per CPU, so the last bit can differ between two laptops even though one machine is self-consistent. Two runs on one machine prove nothing about this. *Pinning tests:* the `detmath` accuracy tests (Task 6), plus a lint test forbidding libm/`**`/numpy/`random` on the lockstep path and a committed golden digest (Task 17). One residual is documented and allow-listed by name in the lint: `ActuatorCore.run_steering_control` (production code, moved verbatim) calls `math.tan`/`math.atan` on the steering command, but its output is truncated to an integer PWM and the straight track commands about zero steering; the golden digests and the CPU-stress test are the net. `test_the_allow_list_is_the_steering_code_and_nothing_wider` pins that the exception covers exactly those two calls in that one file.
5. **Steering scale mismatch between actuator and plant.** actuator.yaml's −1000 counts/rad saturates the servo at 0.05 rad. If the plant inverted PWM with a different ratio than the actuator used, the car would turn by the wrong angle with no error raised. Both read `tire_angle_to_steer_ratio` from `config/actuator_rc_sim.param.yaml`. *Pinning tests:* the config-sync test (Task 5), `test_full_steer_pwm_reaches_lock` (Task 10) and `test_lockstep_uses_rc_sim_ratio` (Task 14).
6. **Motor braking is a second brake, and it must be capped like the first.** A forward command whose duty is below the back-EMF (any PWM just above the deadband while the car is fast) makes a synchronous-rectifying ESC regenerate. Through the motor's 0.06 Ω an uncapped model draws about 160 A at 11 m/s, which is 7 N·m at the rear axle: the rear wheels lock whatever the student does, and the lock and stop checks become unpassable on every variant. The model limits the ESC's current when it drives (`esc_current_limit`, 120 A) and when it opposes the duty (`esc_regen_current_limit`, 8.65 A, which is 0.399 N·m, no more than `brake_torque_max`), so regeneration loads the rear tyres no more than the ESC brake does. *Pinning tests:* `test_motoring_and_regeneration_stay_within_the_esc_limits` and `test_motor_braking_stays_under_the_rear_grip` (Task 8), `test_motor_braking_under_a_forward_command_does_not_lock_the_rear_wheels` on every variant and its contrast `test_uncapped_motor_braking_would_lock_the_rear_wheels` (Task 11).

## Spec Findings Settled in This Plan

Read these before reviewing the tasks. Each one is a place where the code disagreed with the spec's arithmetic or left a choice open.

- **Loaded top speed is ≈ 12.2 m/s, not 11.** With R = 0.06 Ω, the drive force falls 12.9 N per m/s. The force balance at 11 m/s would need about 21 N of resistance, four times the ≈ 5 N that rolling resistance (0.05), drag and drivetrain losses give. The plan keeps the mock motor and those losses, which gives a loaded top speed of ≈ 12.2 m/s. This is the right outcome anyway: a car whose top speed is exactly 11 m/s cannot *regulate* an 11 m/s hold. The test pins the simulated top speed to the analytic force balance and to the band [11.5, 12.6] m/s. Every model variant must reach at least 11.4 m/s (`kv_low` does, at 11.6).
- **Brake endpoint** (Review Focus 1): 360 = full brake; 340 saturates.
- **d = 3.5 m/s² is beyond what rear-only braking can give; d = 2.5 m/s² is decided.** With the brake on the rear axle only, the rear tyres at their peak, load transfer and road load, the most the car can decelerate is about 3.4 m/s² at low speed on μ 0.8, and 3.25 m/s² on μ 0.75 (`drivetrain.rear_brake_limit`), and the ESC's full brake (0.40 N·m, sized so that the rear tyre force stays 10 % under the nominal rear grip at 1, 6 and 11 m/s) gives 3.0-3.5 m/s². A rear-braked car therefore could not follow 3.5 m/s² to the stop line (`test_feasibility_warns_about_rear_braking` shows the generator warning about it on the heavy car, 1.6 m past the line). The user decided d = 2.5 m/s². The straight grows to 115.4 m, every variant can follow the braking ramp (`test_every_variant_follows_a_2_5_decel`), and the stop-error limit is a plain 0.5 m with no per-variant allowance.
- **ESC current limits.** The mock motor would draw 185 A at stall and about 160 A regenerating at 11 m/s, which no 1/10-scale ESC delivers. The model limits the current when driving (120 A, so full throttle from rest gives 105 N at the ground, still far above the rear grip) and when regenerating (8.65 A; Review Focus 6). Both are `guess` values to refit from car data.
- **Stiction matters at only one count.** With the mock motor's 0.06 Ω, any duty gives a large standstill torque. The Karnopp breakaway (0.25 N·m) therefore holds the car only at the first count above the deadband (PWM 376, 0.18 N·m at the axle). "A command that holds a crawl cannot start the car" is true at that count and no higher. The test pins exactly that, and shows the contrast on a car with lighter rolling resistance (0.02), where the same command creeps off without stiction. On the nominal car the running friction (rolling resistance 0.05 plus the drivetrain's Coulomb drag) is already about as large as the 0.18 N·m the first count gives, so removing stiction there would change little.
- **`pwm_output_alpha` has no effect today.** `actuator.py` reads it into `self.pwm_output_alpha`, and nothing uses that value. The grader accepts the key, a test pins that it has no effect, and the guide says so. Making it work would change the car's behaviour; that is the user's call and is left open.
- **The target is position-based.** Autoware's controller commands the trajectory speed at the car's position, so the harness does the same. A pure function of position is 0 at the start line, so accelerating segments use `max(v(s), 0.25 m/s)` (the motion velocity smoother's `engage_velocity`).
- **lf + lr must equal the wheelbase.** The spec table's 0.15 / 0.17 m sums to 0.32 m, against 0.319 m in vehicle_info. lf = 0.15 is a parameter and lr = wheel_base − lf = 0.169 m is derived.
- **The hall sensor is on a rear wheel**, according to docs/guides/lab2-waypoint-stack.md ("spin a rear wheel by hand").
- **`planning_simulator.launch.xml` cannot drop its vehicle simulator.** Its `vehicle_simulation:=false` still launches `simple_planning_simulator`, which only `scenario_simulation` removes. `rc_sim.launch.xml` therefore composes `autoware.launch.xml` with `tier4_simulator_component.launch.xml` and `launch_dummy_vehicle:=false`.
- **Bench-plant retirement is deferred.** `vehicle_bench_plant` still drives the Lab 2 Part 2 bench (markers, EKF), and `motor_plant.py` is the oracle of `test_speed_controller_equivalence.py`. Retiring them means moving the Part 2 bench onto this plant, which belongs in a follow-up plan. Nothing here deletes them.
- **The low-μ variant is 0.75, not 0.6.** At μ = 0.6 the rear tyres cannot give the profile's 2.5 m/s² (the grip-limited launch gives 2.37 m/s²), so every submission would fail the reach checks. At 0.7 the ESC's full brake, sized to the nominal grip, is beyond the rear tyre's: a controller that asks for it locks the wheels, and the lock check fails for reasons that are the model's. 0.75 is the lowest round value that keeps the full brake under the grip at every speed (by 5 % at 11 m/s). The same limit sets the other variants: `light` is 3.3 kg (3.0 kg locked the rear wheels at full brake), `heavy` 3.6 kg, and `deadband_wide` 7 counts (8 spins the wheels on the resume from rest).
- **A speed controller cannot be both aggressive enough to stop at the line and gentle enough on the resume.** With the plain 0.5 m stop-error limit and the 0.5 m/s resume-slip limit, the two checks pull the integral gain in opposite directions: the stop error falls as `ki_speed` rises, and the wheel spin on the resume rises with it. The best coefficients the search found leave the worst variant at 81 % of the stop limit and 84 % of the resume-slip limit (Task 16), so the reference has a margin of about 15 %, no more.
- **The low-speed blend has no relaxation time.** CommonRoad's blend relaxes the lateral and yaw states toward the kinematic bicycle over 0.02 s. When the car stops faster than that (3 m/s² takes 0.1 s per 0.3 m/s), the yaw rate outlives the speed and the car pirouettes at standstill, which the spec's stability requirement forbids (`test_low_speed_blend_is_stable_at_full_lock`). The model blends the states themselves at the step's new speed, so the yaw rate follows the speed to zero; the `ks_relax` parameter is gone.
- **A lurch is an acceleration over 50 ms, not over 1 ms.** The plant's per-millisecond `ax` carries the contact impulses of the Karnopp stick (hundreds of m/s² at the instant a wheel releases). `launch_accel` therefore uses the speed change over a 50 ms window (`grade.LAUNCH_ACCEL_WINDOW`).

## Task 1: `max_pwm_offset` in `AckermannSpeedPID` (submodule)

The production controller limits its output to `init_pwm ± (max_pwm − min_pwm) // 2` = 370 ± 55, which tops out at PWM 425 (about 6.4 m/s on the model). This task makes the limit a coefficient. Unset, nothing changes.

**Files:**
- Modify: `src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch/autosdv_vehicle_interface/autosdv_vehicle_interface/speed_controller.py`
- Modify: `src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch/autosdv_vehicle_interface/test/test_speed_controller.py`
- Modify: `src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch/autosdv_vehicle_interface/README.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `params: Dict[str, Any]` as the actuator builds it (`init_pwm`, `min_pwm`, `max_pwm`, the `*_speed` gains, …), merged with `speed_controller_params`.
- Produces: `AckermannSpeedPID(params)` reads optional `params["max_pwm_offset"]: int`. Unset: `(max_pwm − min_pwm) // 2`. Set: `min(value, max_pwm − init_pwm)`. ≤ 0: `ValueError`. Read back as `controller.max_pwm_offset: int`.

- [ ] **Step 0: Put the submodule on its branch**

```bash
cd /home/aeon/repos/AutoSDV/src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch
git checkout 1.5.0 && git pull --rebase origin 1.5.0
```

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing tests** (append to `test/test_speed_controller.py`; `PARAMS` is already defined there)

```python
def test_max_pwm_offset_unset_keeps_today_s_limit():
    c = AckermannSpeedPID(dict(PARAMS))
    assert c.max_pwm_offset == 55
    assert (c.pid.min_output, c.pid.max_output) == (-55, 55)
    assert c.update(20.0, 0.0, 0.01) == 425   # saturated: 370 + 55
    c.reset()
    assert c.update(-20.0, 5.0, 0.01) == 315


def test_max_pwm_offset_100_reaches_full_throttle():
    c = AckermannSpeedPID(dict(PARAMS, max_pwm_offset=100))
    assert c.update(20.0, 0.0, 0.01) == 470


def test_max_pwm_offset_capped_at_the_actuator_clamp():
    c = AckermannSpeedPID(dict(PARAMS, max_pwm_offset=150))
    assert c.max_pwm_offset == 100   # max_pwm - init_pwm
    assert c.update(20.0, 0.0, 0.01) == 470


@pytest.mark.parametrize("bad", [0, -5])
def test_max_pwm_offset_must_be_positive(bad):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="max_pwm_offset"):
        AckermannSpeedPID(dict(PARAMS, max_pwm_offset=bad))


def test_max_pwm_offset_through_the_loader():
    c = load_speed_controller(None, dict(PARAMS, max_pwm_offset=80))
    assert c.max_pwm_offset == 80
```


- [ ] **Step 3: Implement.** In `speed_controller.py`, replace the head of `AckermannSpeedPID.__init__` (the two lines computing `pwm_range` and `max_pwm_offset`) with the code below, and add the paragraph to the class docstring after "Reads …":

```python
    def __init__(self, params: Dict[str, Any]):
        super().__init__(params)
        init_pwm = int(params["init_pwm"])
        max_pwm = int(params["max_pwm"])
        min_pwm = int(params["min_pwm"])
        if params.get("max_pwm_offset") is None:
            # Unset: the limit this controller has always had, unchanged
            max_pwm_offset = (max_pwm - min_pwm) // 2
        else:
            max_pwm_offset = int(params["max_pwm_offset"])
            if max_pwm_offset <= 0:
                raise ValueError(f"max_pwm_offset must be positive, got {max_pwm_offset}")
            # The shell clamps to max_pwm anyway; a larger limit only winds up
            max_pwm_offset = min(max_pwm_offset, max_pwm - init_pwm)
        self.max_pwm_offset = max_pwm_offset

        self.init_pwm = init_pwm
```

(keep the rest of `__init__`, starting at `self.pid = AckermannPID(`, unchanged; it already uses the local `max_pwm_offset`.)

Docstring paragraph:

```
    ``max_pwm_offset`` (optional) is the output limit around ``init_pwm`` in
    counts. Unset, it is ``(max_pwm - min_pwm) // 2`` (55 with actuator.yaml),
    which tops out at PWM 425; set, it is capped at ``max_pwm - init_pwm``.
```

```python
"""Hardware backends: real and simulated PCA9685 and GPIO. No I2C, no GPIO, no ROS graph."""
import sys

import pytest

from autosdv_vehicle_interface import hal


class FakePub:
    def __init__(self, topic):
        self.topic = topic
        self.sent = []

    def publish(self, msg):
        self.sent.append(list(msg.data))


class FakeNode:
    def __init__(self):
        self.pubs = []
        self.subs = []

    def create_publisher(self, msg_type, topic, qos):
        pub = FakePub(topic)
        self.pubs.append(pub)
        return pub

    def create_subscription(self, msg_type, topic, callback, qos):
        self.subs.append((topic, callback))
        return object()


def test_check_hardware():
    assert hal.check_hardware("real") == "real"
    assert hal.check_hardware("sim") == "sim"
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="hardware"):
        hal.check_hardware("simulation")


def test_sim_pca9685_forwards_each_write():
    sent = []
    drv = hal.SimPCA9685(lambda ch, value: sent.append((ch, value)))
    drv.set_pwm_freq(60)
    drv.set_pwm(0, 0, 425)
    drv.set_pwm(1, 0, 489)
    assert drv.freq == 60
    assert sent == [(0, 425), (1, 489)]


def test_make_pwm_driver_real_uses_the_given_class(fake_pca):
    params = {"hardware": "real", "i2c_address": 64, "i2c_busnum": 7, "pwm_freq": 60}
    drv = hal.make_pwm_driver(params, pca_class=fake_pca)
    assert (drv.address, drv.busnum, drv.freq) == (64, 7, 60)


def test_make_pwm_driver_sim_publishes_on_the_pwm_topic():
    pytest.importorskip("std_msgs")
    node = FakeNode()
    params = {"hardware": "sim", "i2c_address": 64, "i2c_busnum": 7, "pwm_freq": 60}
    drv = hal.make_pwm_driver(params, node=node)
    drv.set_pwm(0, 0, 400)
    assert isinstance(drv, hal.SimPCA9685)
    assert node.pubs[0].topic == hal.PWM_TOPIC
    assert node.pubs[0].sent == [[0, 400]]


def test_sim_gpio_delivers_every_edge_with_its_stamp():
    pytest.importorskip("std_msgs")
    from std_msgs.msg import Int64MultiArray
    node = FakeNode()
    gpio = hal.make_gpio("sim", node=node)
    got = []
    gpio.setmode(gpio.BOARD)
    gpio.setup(15, gpio.IN)
    gpio.add_event_detect(15, gpio.RISING, callback=lambda ch, stamp_ns: got.append((ch, stamp_ns)))
    topic, callback = node.subs[0]
    assert topic == hal.EDGE_TOPIC
    callback(Int64MultiArray(data=[1_000, 2_500]))
    assert got == [(15, 1_000), (15, 2_500)]


def test_real_gpio_is_imported_only_when_asked(monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "Jetson", None)       # no Jetson.GPIO on this machine
    monkeypatch.delitem(sys.modules, "Jetson.GPIO", raising=False)
    with pytest.raises(ImportError):
        hal.make_gpio("real")
    assert isinstance(hal.make_gpio("sim", node=FakeNode()), hal.SimGPIO)
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** — `autosdv_vehicle_interface/hal.py`

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
The vehicle interface's two hardware touchpoints, and their simulated backends.

On the car the actuator writes PWM through an Adafruit PCA9685 and
velocity_report times hall-sensor edges through Jetson.GPIO. With
``hardware: sim`` the same nodes run against the autosdv_rc_sim plant
instead, and nothing else in them changes:

    actuator ── set_pwm ──> SimPCA9685 ── /rc_sim/pwm ──> plant
    plant ── /rc_sim/hall_edges ──> SimGPIO ── callback(pin, stamp_ns) ──> velocity_report

Each simulated edge carries its true time on the simulation clock, so
message latency does not become speed noise. The hardware libraries are
imported only on the real path, so the package imports on a laptop.
"""
from typing import Callable

HARDWARE_REAL = "real"
HARDWARE_SIM = "sim"
PWM_TOPIC = "/rc_sim/pwm"
EDGE_TOPIC = "/rc_sim/hall_edges"


def check_hardware(value: str) -> str:
    """Return ``value`` if it names a backend, else raise ValueError."""
    if value not in (HARDWARE_REAL, HARDWARE_SIM):
        raise ValueError(
            f"hardware must be '{HARDWARE_REAL}' or '{HARDWARE_SIM}', got {value!r}")
    return value


class SimPCA9685:
    """Stand-in for ``Adafruit_PCA9685.PCA9685``: the two methods the actuator calls."""

    def __init__(self, publish: Callable[[int, int], None]):
        self._publish = publish
        self.freq = None

    def set_pwm_freq(self, freq) -> None:
        self.freq = int(freq)

    def set_pwm(self, channel, on, off) -> None:
        self._publish(int(channel), int(off))


class SimGPIO:
    """Stand-in for the ``Jetson.GPIO`` module: the three calls velocity_report makes."""

    BOARD = "BOARD"
    IN = "IN"
    RISING = "RISING"

    def __init__(self, node, topic: str = EDGE_TOPIC):
        self._node = node
        self._topic = topic
        self._subscriptions = []

    def setmode(self, mode) -> None:
        pass

    def setup(self, pin, direction) -> None:
        pass

    def add_event_detect(self, pin, edge, callback) -> None:
        from rclpy.qos import QoSProfile, ReliabilityPolicy
        from std_msgs.msg import Int64MultiArray

        def on_edges(msg):
            for stamp_ns in msg.data:
                callback(pin, int(stamp_ns))

        qos = QoSProfile(depth=100, reliability=ReliabilityPolicy.RELIABLE)
        self._subscriptions.append(
            self._node.create_subscription(Int64MultiArray, self._topic, on_edges, qos))


def make_pwm_driver(params: dict, node=None, pca_class=None):
    """Return the PWM driver for ``params["hardware"]`` (dry runs are the caller's business)."""
    hardware = check_hardware(params.get("hardware", HARDWARE_REAL))
    if hardware == HARDWARE_SIM:
        from rclpy.qos import QoSProfile, ReliabilityPolicy
        from std_msgs.msg import Int32MultiArray
        qos = QoSProfile(depth=10, reliability=ReliabilityPolicy.RELIABLE)
        publisher = node.create_publisher(Int32MultiArray, PWM_TOPIC, qos)

        def publish(channel: int, value: int) -> None:
            publisher.publish(Int32MultiArray(data=[channel, value]))

        driver = SimPCA9685(publish)
    else:
        if pca_class is None:
            from Adafruit_PCA9685 import PCA9685 as pca_class
        driver = pca_class(address=params["i2c_address"], busnum=params["i2c_busnum"])
    driver.set_pwm_freq(params["pwm_freq"])
    return driver


def make_gpio(hardware: str, node=None):
    """Return the GPIO module for ``hardware``: Jetson.GPIO, or a SimGPIO on ``node``."""
    if check_hardware(hardware) == HARDWARE_SIM:
        return SimGPIO(node)
    import Jetson.GPIO as GPIO
    return GPIO
```

The FakeNode in the test ignores the QoS argument, so passing a real `QoSProfile` is fine there. On the sim path `rclpy` is imported only when a `SimGPIO`/sim driver is actually built.

```python
"""VelocityEstimator against the bench's independent copy of the node's arithmetic."""
import math

from autosdv_vehicle_interface.motor_plant import HallSpeedSensor
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.velocity_estimator import VelocityEstimator
import pytest

D = 0.105 * math.pi / 12


def edge_train():
    """Edges of a wheel accelerating to 3 m/s, then braking to a crawl and stopping."""
    t_ns, v, out = 1_000_000_000, 0.2, []
    for i in range(400):
        v = min(3.0, v + 0.02) if i < 300 else max(0.05, v - 0.2)
        t_ns += int(round(D / v * 1e9))
        out.append(t_ns)
    return out


def test_matches_the_bench_hall_model():
    est, ref = VelocityEstimator(D), HallSpeedSensor()
    edges = edge_train()
    i = 0
    for now in range(1_000_000_000, edges[-1] + 2_000_000_000, 50_000_000):
        while i < len(edges) and edges[i] <= now:
            est.on_edge(edges[i])
            ref._on_edge(edges[i] / 1e9)
            i += 1
        assert est.report(now) == pytest.approx(ref.report(now / 1e9), rel=1e-9, abs=1e-12)


def test_zero_before_any_edge_and_after_the_timeout():
    est = VelocityEstimator(D)
    assert est.report(10**9) == 0.0
    est.on_edge(10**9)
    est.on_edge(10**9 + 20_000_000)
    assert est.report(10**9 + 25_000_000) > 0.0
    assert est.report(10**9 + 20_000_000 + 600_000_000) == 0.0


def test_edges_closer_than_1ms_are_ignored():
    est = VelocityEstimator(D)
    est.on_edge(0)
    est.on_edge(10_000_000)
    v = est.current_velocity
    est.on_edge(10_500_000)
    assert est.current_velocity == v
    assert est.last_edge_ns == 10_500_000
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement** — `autosdv_vehicle_interface/velocity_estimator.py`

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Wheel speed from hall-sensor edges: velocity_report's arithmetic, without ROS.

Shared by the velocity_report node and the rc_sim lockstep grader, so a
graded run measures speed with the car's code. Times are integer
nanoseconds, the unit of the ROS clock, which keeps the arithmetic identical
to the node's ``(now - last).nanoseconds / 10**9``.

- Each edge gives an instantaneous speed ``distance_per_marker / dt``
  (edges closer than 1 ms are ignored), smoothed by an EMA (alpha 0.3).
- ``gap_bound``: no edge for ``since`` seconds means the wheel rolled less
  than one marker spacing in that time, so the estimate is capped at
  ``overdue_slack * distance_per_marker / since``. Without it the estimate
  holds its last value until the timeout after the wheel stops.
- No edge for ``timeout`` seconds: zero.

The sensor has no direction: the result is never negative.
"""
from typing import Optional


class VelocityEstimator:
    """Edge-to-edge wheel speed, as published by velocity_report."""

    MIN_EDGE_DT = 0.001  # [s] shorter gaps are noise (about 27 m/s for this wheel)

    def __init__(self, distance_per_marker: float, alpha: float = 0.3,
                 timeout: float = 0.5, overdue_slack: float = 1.5):
        self.distance_per_marker = distance_per_marker
        self.alpha = alpha
        self.timeout = timeout
        self.overdue_slack = overdue_slack
        self.last_edge_ns: Optional[int] = None
        self.current_velocity = 0.0

    def gap_bound(self, now_ns: int) -> float:
        """Cap the estimate at what the time since the last edge allows."""
        if self.last_edge_ns is None:
            return self.current_velocity
        since = (now_ns - self.last_edge_ns) / (10**9)
        if since <= 0.0:
            return self.current_velocity
        return min(self.current_velocity,
                   self.overdue_slack * self.distance_per_marker / since)

    def on_edge(self, t_ns: int) -> None:
        """Register a marker edge at ``t_ns``."""
        if self.last_edge_ns is not None:
            dt = (t_ns - self.last_edge_ns) / (10**9)
            if dt > self.MIN_EDGE_DT:
                instantaneous = self.distance_per_marker / dt
                # Start from the capped estimate, so a slowdown the cap already
                # showed does not bounce back up through the EMA
                self.current_velocity = self.gap_bound(t_ns)
                if self.current_velocity == 0.0:
                    self.current_velocity = instantaneous
                else:
                    self.current_velocity = (
                        self.alpha * instantaneous
                        + (1.0 - self.alpha) * self.current_velocity
                    )
        self.last_edge_ns = t_ns

    def report(self, now_ns: int) -> float:
        """Return the speed published at ``now_ns`` (applies the timeout, then the cap)."""
        if self.last_edge_ns is not None:
            if (now_ns - self.last_edge_ns) / (10**9) > self.timeout:
                self.current_velocity = 0.0
        return self.gap_bound(now_ns)
```

```python
"""velocity_report with hardware:=sim: the node and a bare VelocityEstimator agree on the same edges.

Needs rclpy (skipped without). Run under ROS_DOMAIN_ID=73 and flock tmp/bench.lock.
"""
import os
import sys
import time

import pytest

os.environ.setdefault("ROS_DOMAIN_ID", "73")
rclpy = pytest.importorskip("rclpy")

# Imported plainly, not skipped: before Step 6 this import is the red phase on
# any machine without Jetson.GPIO
from autosdv_vehicle_interface import hal  # noqa: E402
from autosdv_vehicle_interface import velocity_report  # noqa: E402
from rclpy.parameter import Parameter  # noqa: E402
from std_msgs.msg import Int64MultiArray  # noqa: E402

from autosdv_vehicle_interface.hal import EDGE_TOPIC  # noqa: E402
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.velocity_estimator import VelocityEstimator  # noqa: E402

PARAMS = {"pin": 15, "rate": 20.0, "wheel_diameter": 10.5, "markers_per_rotation": 12,
          "frame_id": "base_link", "hardware": "sim"}


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def ros():
    rclpy.init()
    yield
    rclpy.try_shutdown()


def spin_until(node, predicate, timeout=10.0):
    deadline = time.monotonic() + timeout
    while time.monotonic() < deadline:
        if predicate():
            return True
        rclpy.spin_once(node, timeout_sec=0.05)
    return predicate()


def test_sim_edges_give_the_estimators_value(ros):
    node = velocity_report.AutoSdvVelocityReportNode(
        parameter_overrides=[Parameter(k, value=v) for k, v in PARAMS.items()])
    pub_node = rclpy.create_node("rc_sim_edge_pub")
    pub = pub_node.create_publisher(Int64MultiArray, EDGE_TOPIC, 100)
    try:
        assert spin_until(node, lambda: pub.get_subscription_count() > 0)
        d = node.distance_per_marker
        t, edges = 1_000_000_000, []
        for i in range(300):
            t += int(round(d / (0.3 + 0.01 * i) * 1e9))
            edges.append(t)
        ref = VelocityEstimator(d)
        for i in range(0, len(edges), 10):
            batch = edges[i:i + 10]
            pub.publish(Int64MultiArray(data=batch))
            for e in batch:
                ref.on_edge(e)
        assert spin_until(node, lambda: node.estimator.last_edge_ns == edges[-1])
        assert node.estimator.current_velocity == ref.current_velocity
        later = edges[-1] + 30_000_000
        assert node.estimator.report(later) == ref.report(later)
    finally:
        pub_node.destroy_node()
        node.destroy_node()


def test_imports_without_jetson_gpio(ros, monkeypatch):
    """The module imports with Jetson.GPIO unavailable, and hardware:=sim selects SimGPIO."""
    import importlib
    monkeypatch.setitem(sys.modules, "Jetson", None)       # importing Jetson.GPIO now raises
    monkeypatch.delitem(sys.modules, "Jetson.GPIO", raising=False)
    module = importlib.reload(velocity_report)
    assert module.AutoSdvVelocityReportNode.__name__ == "AutoSdvVelocityReportNode"
    assert "Jetson.GPIO" not in sys.modules
    node = rclpy.create_node("rc_sim_gpio_probe")
    try:
        assert isinstance(module.make_gpio("sim", node=node), hal.SimGPIO)
    finally:
        node.destroy_node()
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Velocity report node for AutoSDV.

Measures the vehicle's speed from a rear wheel's hall-sensor edges. The
arithmetic is VelocityEstimator's (velocity_estimator.py), shared with the
rc_sim lockstep grader; this node supplies the edges and the clock.

``hardware``: ``real`` (Jetson.GPIO, the default) or ``sim`` (SimGPIO: edges
from the autosdv_rc_sim plant, each carrying its true time).
"""
import math
import sys

import rclpy
from rclpy import Parameter
from rclpy.node import Node
from autoware_vehicle_msgs.msg import VelocityReport

from autosdv_vehicle_interface.hal import HARDWARE_REAL, make_gpio
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.velocity_estimator import VelocityEstimator


class AutoSdvVelocityReportNode(Node):
    """Publish VelocityReport messages from hall-sensor edge timing."""

    def __init__(self, **kwargs) -> None:
        super().__init__("autosdv_velocity_report_node", **kwargs)

        self.declare_parameter("pin", Parameter.Type.INTEGER)
        self.declare_parameter("rate", Parameter.Type.DOUBLE)
        self.declare_parameter("wheel_diameter", Parameter.Type.DOUBLE)
        self.declare_parameter("markers_per_rotation", Parameter.Type.INTEGER)
        self.declare_parameter("frame_id", Parameter.Type.STRING)
        self.declare_parameter("hardware", HARDWARE_REAL)

        self.publisher = self.create_publisher(VelocityReport, "~/output/velocity_status", 1)

        wheel_diameter_cm = self.get_parameter("wheel_diameter").get_parameter_value().double_value
        wheel_circumference_meters = wheel_diameter_cm / 100.0 * math.pi
        self.wheel_circumference_meters = wheel_circumference_meters
        self.markers_per_rotation = (
            self.get_parameter("markers_per_rotation").get_parameter_value().integer_value
        )
        self.frame_id = self.get_parameter("frame_id").get_parameter_value().string_value
        self.distance_per_marker = wheel_circumference_meters / self.markers_per_rotation
        self.estimator = VelocityEstimator(self.distance_per_marker)
        self.clock = self.get_clock()

        # The hall sensor: Jetson.GPIO on the car, the rc_sim plant's edges in simulation
        self.pin = self.get_parameter("pin").get_parameter_value().integer_value
        self.gpio = make_gpio(self.get_parameter("hardware").value, node=self)
        self.gpio.setmode(self.gpio.BOARD)
        self.gpio.setup(self.pin, self.gpio.IN)
        self.gpio.add_event_detect(self.pin, self.gpio.RISING, callback=self.on_gpio_rising)

        rate = self.get_parameter("rate").get_parameter_value().double_value
        self.timer = self.create_timer(1.0 / rate, self.publish_callback)

    def publish_callback(self) -> None:
        """Publish the current estimate (zero once the edges have stopped)."""
        curr_time = self.clock.now()
        msg = VelocityReport()
        msg.header.stamp = curr_time.to_msg()
        msg.header.frame_id = self.frame_id
        msg.longitudinal_velocity = self.estimator.report(curr_time.nanoseconds)
        msg.lateral_velocity = 0.0
        msg.heading_rate = 0.0
        self.publisher.publish(msg)

    def on_gpio_rising(self, channel, stamp_ns=None) -> None:
        """
        Register a marker edge.

        Args:
            channel: GPIO channel that triggered the event
            stamp_ns: The edge's true time [ns], from a simulated backend; the
                real GPIO callback passes none and the edge is timed on arrival.
        """
        if stamp_ns is None:
            stamp_ns = self.clock.now().nanoseconds
        self.estimator.on_edge(stamp_ns)
```

## Task 4: `ActuatorCore`, the clock source, and `hardware` on the actuator (submodule)

This task moves the actuator's per-tick logic into a ROS-free class so that the lockstep harness can run the car's code. The node then becomes a wrapper. Under `use_sim_time` the node times itself on the ROS clock (Review Focus 2), and it gets its PWM backend from `hal`.

**Files:**
- Create: `…/autosdv_vehicle_interface/autosdv_vehicle_interface/actuator_core.py`
- Modify: `…/autosdv_vehicle_interface/autosdv_vehicle_interface/actuator.py`
- Create: `…/autosdv_vehicle_interface/test/test_actuator_core.py`
- Create: `…/autosdv_vehicle_interface/test/test_actuator_duality.py`
- Modify: `…/autosdv_vehicle_launch/launch/vehicle_interface.launch.xml`
- Modify: `…/autosdv_vehicle_interface/README.md`

**Interfaces:**
- Consumes: `LongitudinalShell`, `ShellConfig`, the `OVERRIDE_*` constants (longitudinal.py), `InputWatchdog`, `InputState` (safety.py), `hal.make_pwm_driver`, `hal.check_hardware`, `hal.HARDWARE_SIM` (Task 2).
- Produces (`actuator_core.py`):
  - `DEFAULTS: Dict[str, Any]`, `FILE_KEY_RENAMES`, `SPEED_CONTROLLER_BASE_PARAMS: tuple`.
  - `normalize_params(raw: dict) -> dict`: YAML keys go to the node's internal names, with `DEFAULTS` filled in.
  - `build_controller_params(params: dict, overrides: Optional[dict] = None) -> dict`.
  - `State`, `Config` (moved from actuator.py and re-exported there), `make_config(params) -> Config`, `make_shell_config(params) -> ShellConfig`.
  - `ActuatorCore(params: dict, controller, logger=None, start_time: float = 0.0)` with `.on_velocity(now, monotonic_now, speed)`, `.on_command(monotonic_now, velocity, tire_angle)`, `.tick(now, monotonic_now) -> Tuple[int, int]` (motor PWM, steering PWM), `.select_override(cmd_state, velocity_state) -> Optional[str]`, `.run_steering_control(delta_time) -> int`, and attributes `config`, `state`, `longitudinal`, `speed_controller`, `cmd_watchdog`, `velocity_watchdog`, `gate_emergency`, `mrm_emergency`.
  - Node: parameter `hardware` (default `real`), `self.core`, `_now()`, `_monotonic()`, and the class attribute `PCA9685` (or `None` when the Adafruit library is missing).

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing pure test** — `test/test_actuator_core.py` (license header as before)

```python
"""ActuatorCore without ROS: the actuator's tick on explicit clocks."""
import os

import yaml

from autosdv_vehicle_interface.actuator_core import (
    ActuatorCore, build_controller_params, normalize_params)
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.longitudinal import Mode
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.speed_controller import load_speed_controller

HERE = os.path.dirname(os.path.abspath(__file__))
PKG = os.path.dirname(HERE)
ACTUATOR_YAML = os.path.join(PKG, "params", "actuator.yaml")
VEHICLE_INFO_YAML = os.path.join(
    PKG, "..", "autosdv_vehicle_description", "config", "vehicle_info.param.yaml")


def file_params(**overrides):
    raw = {}
    for path in (ACTUATOR_YAML, VEHICLE_INFO_YAML):
        with open(path) as f:
            raw.update(yaml.safe_load(f)["/**"]["ros__parameters"])
    raw.update(overrides)
    return normalize_params(raw)


def make_core(**overrides):
    params = file_params(**overrides)
    controller = load_speed_controller(None, build_controller_params(params))
    return ActuatorCore(params, controller, start_time=0.0)


def test_normalize_renames_vehicle_info_keys_and_fills_defaults():
    p = file_params()
    assert p["wheelbase"] == 0.319 and p["track_width"] == 0.263
    assert p["max_steering_angle"] == 0.349
    assert p["control_cmd_timeout"] == 0.3 and p["speed_controller_dt_source"] == "velocity_report"


def test_waiting_until_both_inputs_arrive():
    core = make_core()
    assert core.tick(0.01, 0.01) == (370, 489)
    assert core.longitudinal.mode == Mode.WAITING


def test_drives_forward_then_brakes_on_a_stop_command():
    core = make_core(speed_controller_dt_source="loop")
    t = 0.0
    for k in range(50):
        t = 0.01 * k
        if k % 5 == 0:
            core.on_velocity(t, t, 0.5)
        if k % 3 == 0:
            core.on_command(t, 1.5, 0.0)
        motor, steer = core.tick(t, t)
    assert motor > 375 and steer == 489
    core.on_command(t + 0.01, 0.0, 0.0)
    assert core.tick(t + 0.02, t + 0.02)[0] == 340   # BRAKE: target 0, moving at 0.5 m/s


def test_command_timeout_brakes_then_neutral_and_centres_steering():
    core = make_core()
    core.on_velocity(0.0, 0.0, 1.0)
    core.on_command(0.0, 1.0, 0.1)
    assert core.tick(0.5, 0.5) == (340, 489)        # 0.5 s > control_cmd_timeout 0.3: brake, centred
    assert core.longitudinal.override_reason == "command_timeout"


def test_gate_emergency_overrides_the_controller():
    core = make_core()
    core.on_velocity(0.0, 0.0, 2.0)
    core.on_command(0.0, 2.0, 0.0)
    core.gate_emergency = True
    assert core.tick(0.01, 0.01)[0] == 340
    assert core.longitudinal.override_reason == "emergency"


def test_steering_clamps_to_the_lock_and_maps_through_the_ratio():
    core = make_core(tire_angle_to_steer_ratio=-143.0)
    core.on_velocity(0.0, 0.0, 0.0)
    for k in range(200):                               # let the 0.3 EMA settle
        core.on_command(0.01 * k, 0.0, 1.0)             # far beyond 0.349
        steer = core.tick(0.01 * k, 0.01 * k)[1]
    assert core.state.target_tire_angle == 0.349
    assert steer == 439                                 # min_steer: the lock, clamped
```

```python
#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
The actuator's control logic, free of ROS.

actuator.py wraps this class: it turns ROS messages into the calls below and
writes the returned PWM to the PCA9685. The rc_sim lockstep grader drives the
same class directly, so a graded run is the car's code, tick for tick.

Times are seconds on clocks the caller chooses: ``now`` for the PID's dt and
the shell (the node: wall time, or the ROS clock under use_sim_time), and
``monotonic_now`` for the watchdogs.
"""
from dataclasses import dataclass
import math
from typing import Any, Dict, Optional, Tuple

from autosdv_vehicle_interface.longitudinal import (
    LongitudinalShell,
    OVERRIDE_COMMAND_TIMEOUT,
    OVERRIDE_EMERGENCY,
    OVERRIDE_VELOCITY_TIMEOUT,
    ShellConfig,
)
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.safety import InputState, InputWatchdog
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.speed_controller import DEFAULT_SPEED_CONTROLLER

# Parameters the node declares with a default; the others come from
# actuator.yaml and vehicle_info.param.yaml and have none.
DEFAULTS: Dict[str, Any] = {
    "speed_controller_class": DEFAULT_SPEED_CONTROLLER,
    "speed_controller_params": "",
    "speed_controller_dt_source": "velocity_report",
    "controller_fault_holdoff": 1.0,
    "controller_max_faults": 3,
    "control_cmd_timeout": 0.3,
    "control_cmd_max_stamp_age": 0.5,
    "velocity_timeout": 0.5,
    "override_brake_duration": 1.5,
    "enable_emergency_input": True,
    "emergency_stop_on_any_mrm": False,
    "neutral_write_timeout": 0.5,
}

# vehicle_info.param.yaml's names -> the names this code uses
FILE_KEY_RENAMES = {
    "max_steer_angle": "max_steering_angle",
    "wheel_base": "wheelbase",
    "wheel_tread": "track_width",
}

# Actuator parameters every speed controller receives in its params dict
SPEED_CONTROLLER_BASE_PARAMS = (
    "init_pwm", "min_pwm", "max_pwm", "brake_pwm", "rate",
    "kp_speed", "ki_speed", "kd_speed", "integral_limit",
    "enable_conditional_integration", "derivative_filter_alpha",
)


def normalize_params(raw: Dict[str, Any]) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """Merged parameter files -> the dict the node builds from its parameters."""
    params = dict(DEFAULTS)
    for key, value in raw.items():
        params[FILE_KEY_RENAMES.get(key, key)] = value
    return params


def build_controller_params(params: Dict[str, Any],
                            overrides: Optional[Dict[str, Any]] = None) -> Dict[str, Any]:
    """The speed controller's params: the actuator's base set, overrides winning."""
    controller_params = {name: params[name] for name in SPEED_CONTROLLER_BASE_PARAMS}
    controller_params.update(overrides or {})
    return controller_params


# --- State and Config: moved verbatim from actuator.py --------------------
@dataclass
class State:
    ...  # the existing dataclass, unchanged


@dataclass
class Config:
    ...  # the existing dataclass, unchanged


def make_config(params: Dict[str, Any]) -> Config:
    return Config(
        min_pwm=params["min_pwm"],
        init_pwm=params["init_pwm"],
        max_pwm=params["max_pwm"],
        brake_pwm=params["brake_pwm"],
        min_steer=params["min_steer"],
        init_steer=params["init_steer"],
        max_steer=params["max_steer"],
        tire_angle_to_steer_ratio=params["tire_angle_to_steer_ratio"],
        steering_speed=params["steering_speed"],
        max_steering_angle=params["max_steering_angle"],
        wheelbase=params["wheelbase"],
        track_width=params["track_width"],
    )


def make_shell_config(params: Dict[str, Any]) -> ShellConfig:
    return ShellConfig(
        init_pwm=params["init_pwm"],
        min_pwm=params["min_pwm"],
        max_pwm=params["max_pwm"],
        brake_pwm=params["brake_pwm"],
        full_stop_threshold=params["full_stop_threshold"],
        brake_threshold=params["brake_threshold"],
        velocity_deadband=params["velocity_deadband"],
        override_brake_duration=params["override_brake_duration"],
        controller_fault_holdoff=params["controller_fault_holdoff"],
        controller_max_faults=params["controller_max_faults"],
        dt_source=params["speed_controller_dt_source"],
    )


class ActuatorCore:
    """One actuator tick: watchdogs, overrides, the longitudinal shell, steering."""

    def __init__(self, params: Dict[str, Any], controller, logger=None, start_time: float = 0.0):
        self.config = make_config(params)
        self.speed_controller = controller
        self.longitudinal = LongitudinalShell(make_shell_config(params), controller, logger=logger)
        self.cmd_watchdog = InputWatchdog("control command", params["control_cmd_timeout"], logger)
        self.velocity_watchdog = InputWatchdog("velocity report", params["velocity_timeout"], logger)
        self.vel_meas_alpha = params["velocity_measurement_filter_alpha"]
        self.vel_cmd_alpha = params["velocity_command_filter_alpha"]
        self.gate_emergency = False
        self.mrm_emergency = False
        self.state = State(
            target_speed=None,
            current_speed=None,
            target_tire_angle=None,
            current_tire_angle=None,
            last_speed=0.0,
            last_update_time=start_time,
        )

    def on_velocity(self, now: float, monotonic_now: float, speed: float) -> None:
        """A velocity report arrived."""
        self.state.last_speed = (
            self.state.current_speed if self.state.current_speed is not None else 0.0
        )
        self.state.current_speed = speed
        self.state.last_update_time = now
        self.velocity_watchdog.feed(monotonic_now)

    def on_command(self, monotonic_now: float, velocity: float, tire_angle: float) -> None:
        """A usable control command arrived (the node validates stamps and finiteness)."""
        self.cmd_watchdog.feed(monotonic_now)
        self.state.target_speed = velocity
        max_angle = self.config.max_steering_angle
        self.state.target_tire_angle = max(-max_angle, min(tire_angle, max_angle))

    def select_override(self, cmd_state: InputState, velocity_state: InputState) -> Optional[str]:
        """Return the reason the motor must stop regardless of the command, or None."""
        if self.gate_emergency or self.mrm_emergency:
            return OVERRIDE_EMERGENCY
        if cmd_state == InputState.TIMED_OUT:
            return OVERRIDE_COMMAND_TIMEOUT
        if velocity_state == InputState.TIMED_OUT:
            return OVERRIDE_VELOCITY_TIMEOUT
        return None

    def tick(self, now: float, monotonic_now: float) -> Tuple[int, int]:
        """Run one control tick; return (motor PWM, steering PWM)."""
        delta_time = now - self.state.last_update_time

        if self.state.current_speed is not None:
            raw = self.state.current_speed
            if self.state.filtered_measured_velocity == 0.0:
                self.state.filtered_measured_velocity = raw
            else:
                self.state.filtered_measured_velocity = (
                    self.vel_meas_alpha * raw
                    + (1.0 - self.vel_meas_alpha) * self.state.filtered_measured_velocity
                )
        if self.state.target_speed is not None:
            raw = self.state.target_speed
            if self.state.filtered_target_velocity == 0.0:
                self.state.filtered_target_velocity = raw
            else:
                self.state.filtered_target_velocity = (
                    self.vel_cmd_alpha * raw
                    + (1.0 - self.vel_cmd_alpha) * self.state.filtered_target_velocity
                )

        cmd_state = self.cmd_watchdog.check(monotonic_now)
        velocity_state = self.velocity_watchdog.check(monotonic_now)
        override = self.select_override(cmd_state, velocity_state)

        if cmd_state == InputState.TIMED_OUT:
            steer_value = self.config.init_steer
        else:
            steer_value = self.run_steering_control(delta_time)

        current_speed = (
            None if velocity_state == InputState.TIMED_OUT else self.state.current_speed
        )
        pwm_value = self.longitudinal.step(
            now, self.state.target_speed, current_speed, self.state.last_update_time, override)
        return pwm_value, steer_value

    def run_steering_control(self, delta_time: float) -> int:
        ...  # moved verbatim from AutoSdvActuator.run_steering_control
```

     ```python
         def _now(self) -> float:
             """Seconds for dt and the shell: the ROS clock under use_sim_time, else wall time."""
     ```

  10. Delete `select_override`, `run_steering_control` and `run_longitudinal_control` from the node. After step 11 nothing in the node calls them (`ActuatorCore.tick` does the work), and nothing else in the package does either (check: `git grep -n 'select_override\|run_steering_control\|run_longitudinal_control' -- '*.py'` finds only `actuator_core.py` and its tests); delegating stubs would be dead code. Then remove the imports that became unused (`python3 -m pyflakes autosdv_vehicle_interface/actuator.py` lists them; the `AckermannPID` re-export already carries its `noqa`).
  11. `timer_callback`: replace everything from `# Calculate delta time` through `self.publish_mode()` with

      ```python
              pwm_value, steer_value = self.core.tick(self._now(), self._monotonic())
              self.publish_mode()
      ```

      (the PWM writes and debug publishing below stay as they are).
  12. `initialize_state` is gone. `__init__` must not call it, and the `state` attribute now comes from the core.

- [ ] **Step 6: Write the failing ROS duality tests** — `test/test_actuator_duality.py` (license header as before)

```python
"""The actuator node against ActuatorCore and against its own sim backend; its clock under sim time.

Needs rclpy and the Autoware messages (skipped without). Run under
ROS_DOMAIN_ID=73 and flock tmp/bench.lock.
"""
import os
import time

import pytest

os.environ.setdefault("ROS_DOMAIN_ID", "73")
rclpy = pytest.importorskip("rclpy")
actuator = pytest.importorskip("autosdv_vehicle_interface.actuator")

from autoware_control_msgs.msg import Control  # noqa: E402
from autoware_vehicle_msgs.msg import VelocityReport  # noqa: E402
from rosgraph_msgs.msg import Clock  # noqa: E402

from autosdv_vehicle_interface import hal  # noqa: E402
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.actuator_core import ActuatorCore, build_controller_params  # noqa: E402
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.safety import InputState  # noqa: E402
from autosdv_vehicle_interface.speed_controller import load_speed_controller  # noqa: E402
from test_actuator_core import file_params  # noqa: E402
from test_actuator_node import load_params  # noqa: E402


@pytest.fixture(scope="module")
def ros():
    rclpy.init()
    yield
    rclpy.try_shutdown()


@pytest.fixture
def nodes(ros, fake_pca, monkeypatch):
    monkeypatch.setattr(actuator, "PCA9685", fake_pca)
    made = []

    def _make(**overrides):
        overrides.setdefault("enable_debug_publishing", False)
        node = actuator.AutoSdvActuator(parameter_overrides=load_params(**overrides))
        made.append(node)
        return node

    yield _make
    for node in made:
        node._neutral_written = True
        node.destroy_node()


def script():
    """(tick, velocity report or None, (target, tire angle) or None) for 400 ticks of 10 ms."""
    out = []
    for k in range(400):
        velocity = None if k % 5 else min(2.0, 0.01 * k)
        command = None
        if k % 3 == 0:
            command = (1.5 if k < 250 else 0.0, 0.02 if 100 <= k < 200 else 0.0)
        out.append((k, velocity, command))
    return out


def drive_node(node, t0=1000.0):
    clock = {"t": t0}
    node._now = lambda: clock["t"]
    node._monotonic = lambda: clock["t"]
    node.core.state.last_update_time = t0
    for k, velocity, command in script():
        clock["t"] = t0 + 0.01 * k
        if velocity is not None:
            msg = VelocityReport()
            msg.longitudinal_velocity = velocity
            node.velocity_callback(msg)
        if command is not None:
            msg = Control()
            msg.longitudinal.velocity, msg.lateral.steering_tire_angle = command
            node.control_callback(msg)
        node.timer_callback()


def test_node_and_core_write_identical_pwm(nodes):
    node = nodes(speed_controller_dt_source="loop")
    drive_node(node)
    written = [value for _, value in node.driver.writes]       # motor, steer, motor, steer, ...
    params = file_params(speed_controller_dt_source="loop")
    core = ActuatorCore(params, load_speed_controller(None, build_controller_params(params)),
                        start_time=1000.0)
    expected = []
    for k, velocity, command in script():
        t = 1000.0 + 0.01 * k
        if velocity is not None:
            core.on_velocity(t, t, velocity)
        if command is not None:
            core.on_command(t, *command)
        expected.extend(core.tick(t, t))
    assert written == expected
    assert max(expected[0::2]) > 375                            # it did drive


def test_real_and_sim_backends_see_identical_writes(nodes):
    real = nodes(speed_controller_dt_source="loop")
    sim = nodes(speed_controller_dt_source="loop", hardware="sim")
    assert isinstance(sim.driver, hal.SimPCA9685)
    recorded = []
    sim.driver._publish = lambda channel, value: recorded.append((channel, value))
    drive_node(real)
    drive_node(sim)
    assert recorded == real.driver.writes


def test_unknown_hardware_refuses_to_start(nodes):
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match="hardware"):
        nodes(hardware="simulator")


def test_wall_clock_without_sim_time(nodes):
    node = nodes()
    assert abs(node._now() - time.time()) < 0.5


def test_sim_time_drives_dt_and_watchdogs(nodes):
    node = nodes(use_sim_time=True)
    pub_node = rclpy.create_node("rc_sim_clock_pub")
    pub = pub_node.create_publisher(Clock, "/clock", 10)

    def set_clock(seconds):
        msg = Clock()
        msg.clock.sec = int(seconds)
        msg.clock.nanosec = int(round((seconds - int(seconds)) * 1e9))
        deadline = time.monotonic() + 10.0
        while time.monotonic() < deadline:
            pub.publish(msg)
            rclpy.spin_once(node, timeout_sec=0.05)
            if abs(node._now() - seconds) < 1e-6:
                return
        raise AssertionError(f"the node's clock never reached {seconds}")

    try:
        set_clock(100.0)
        assert node._monotonic() == pytest.approx(100.0)
        msg = VelocityReport()
        node.velocity_callback(msg)
        assert node.core.state.last_update_time == pytest.approx(100.0)
        time.sleep(0.6)                                        # wall time passes, sim time does not
        assert node.core.velocity_watchdog.check(node._monotonic()) == InputState.FRESH
        set_clock(100.6)                                       # 0.6 s of sim time > velocity_timeout
        assert node.core.velocity_watchdog.check(node._monotonic()) == InputState.TIMED_OUT
    finally:
        pub_node.destroy_node()
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Document.** In the vehicle interface `README.md`, add a section:

```markdown
## Hardware backends and the simulation clock

`hardware` (actuator and velocity_report; launch argument of
`vehicle_interface.launch.xml`, default `real`) selects the two hardware
touchpoints: `real` writes PWM through the PCA9685 and times hall edges on
Jetson.GPIO; `sim` writes PWM to `/rc_sim/pwm` and reads hall edges, each
with its true time, from `/rc_sim/hall_edges` (the autosdv_rc_sim plant).
Nothing else changes. `dry_run` still disables PWM output entirely.

With `use_sim_time:=true` the actuator takes every time it uses (the PID's
dt, the watchdogs) from the ROS clock, so a simulator running slower or
faster than real time does not distort the loop. Without it, the wall clock
is used exactly as before.

The per-tick logic lives in `actuator_core.ActuatorCore` and the speed
estimate in `velocity_estimator.VelocityEstimator`; the nodes only wrap them,
which is what lets the rc_sim grader run the car's code without ROS.
```

```python
import os
from glob import glob

from setuptools import setup

package_name = 'autosdv_rc_sim'


def share_tree(root):
    """data_files entries installing every file under `root` into share/<pkg>/<root>."""
    entries = []
    for directory in sorted({os.path.dirname(p) for p in glob(f'{root}/**/*', recursive=True)
                             if os.path.isfile(p)}):
        files = sorted(p for p in glob(f'{directory}/*') if os.path.isfile(p))
        entries.append((os.path.join('share', package_name, directory), files))
    return entries


setup(
    name=package_name,
    version='0.1.0',
    packages=[package_name],
    package_data={package_name: ['vehicle.yaml']},
    data_files=[
        ('share/ament_index/resource_index/packages', ['resource/' + package_name]),
        ('share/' + package_name, ['package.xml']),
        (os.path.join('share', package_name, 'launch'), glob('launch/*.launch.xml')),
        (os.path.join('share', package_name, 'config'), glob('config/*.yaml')),
    ] + share_tree('tracks'),
    install_requires=['setuptools'],
    zip_safe=True,
    maintainer='aeon',
    maintainer_email='jerry73204@gmail.com',
    description='AutoSDV RC car simulator: PWM-level plant, lockstep grader, track generator.',
    license='Apache-2.0',
    tests_require=['pytest'],
    entry_points={
        'console_scripts': [
            'rc_sim_plant = autosdv_rc_sim.plant_node:main',
            'rc_sim_replay = autosdv_rc_sim.replay_node:main',
            'rc_sim_grade = autosdv_rc_sim.grade:main',
            'rc_sim_make_track = autosdv_rc_sim.track:main',
            'rc_sim_fit = autosdv_rc_sim.fit:main',
        ],
    },
)
```

```yaml
# Actuator overrides for the RC simulator, loaded after actuator.yaml through
# vehicle_interface.launch.xml's actuator_param_overrides (and read by the
# lockstep grader).
#
# tire_angle_to_steer_ratio: actuator.yaml's -1000 saturates the servo at
# 0.05 rad (docs/known-config-defects.md section 8), a defect to be measured
# on the car, not a property to simulate. -143 maps the servo's +-50 counts
# onto vehicle_info's +-0.349 rad lock, as Lab 2's actuator_lab2.param.yaml
# does. The plant's servo reads the same value (vehicle.yaml, source: config),
# so the actuator and the model cannot disagree.
/**:
  ros__parameters:
    tire_angle_to_steer_ratio: -143.0
    enable_debug_publishing: true
```

```python
# test/conftest.py
"""Put the source trees first on sys.path, so the tests run on the code in this checkout.

Temporary directories go under ./tmp, not the system /tmp (the repository's rule).
"""
from pathlib import Path
import sys

import pytest

REPO = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[4]
PKG = REPO / 'src/sim/autosdv_rc_sim'
VEHICLE_INTERFACE = REPO / 'src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch/autosdv_vehicle_interface'
for path in (str(VEHICLE_INTERFACE), str(PKG)):
    if path in sys.path:
        sys.path.remove(path)
    sys.path.insert(0, path)


@pytest.hookimpl(tryfirst=True)
def pytest_configure(config):
    if config.option.basetemp is None:
        parent = REPO / 'tmp' / 'pytest'
        parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
        config.option.basetemp = parent / 'rc_sim'
```

```python
# test/test_params.py
"""vehicle.yaml: complete, every value tagged, config values equal to their files."""
import dataclasses
import math

from conftest import REPO
import pytest
import yaml

from autosdv_rc_sim.params import (
    DEFAULT_FILE, GRAVITY, load_tagged, load_vehicle, SOURCES, VehicleParams, variant_names)

PKG_DIRS = {
    'autosdv_vehicle_description': REPO / 'src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch/autosdv_vehicle_description',
    'autosdv_vehicle_interface': REPO / 'src/vehicle/autosdv_vehicle_launch/autosdv_vehicle_interface',
    'autosdv_rc_sim': REPO / 'src/sim/autosdv_rc_sim',
}


def test_every_field_is_present_and_tagged():
    tagged = load_tagged()
    names = {f.name for f in dataclasses.fields(VehicleParams)}
    assert set(tagged.entries) == names
    for key, entry in tagged.entries.items():
        assert entry['source'] in SOURCES, key


def test_config_values_match_their_files():
    checked = 0
    for key, entry in load_tagged().entries.items():
        if entry['source'] != 'config':
            continue
        ref, _, param = entry['from'].rpartition(':')
        pkg, _, rel = ref.partition('/')
        with open(PKG_DIRS[pkg] / rel) as f:
            ros = yaml.safe_load(f)['/**']['ros__parameters']
        assert ros[param] * entry.get('scale', 1.0) == pytest.approx(entry['value'], rel=1e-12), key
        checked += 1
    assert checked >= 13


def test_derived_values():
    p = load_vehicle()
    assert p.lr == pytest.approx(0.169)
    assert p.kt == pytest.approx(60.0 / (2.0 * math.pi * 3000.0)) == p.ke
    assert p.rear_axle_inertia == pytest.approx(1.4e-4 + 1.0e-6 * 14.5 * 14.5)
    assert p.fz_front_static + p.fz_rear_static == pytest.approx(p.mass * GRAVITY)
    assert p.b_lat_front * p.pacejka_c * p.mu * p.fz_front_static == pytest.approx(94.0)


def test_motor_braking_is_capped_at_the_esc_brake():
    p = load_vehicle()
    assert p.gear_ratio * p.kt * p.esc_regen_current_limit <= p.brake_torque_max
    assert p.esc_regen_current_limit < p.esc_current_limit


def test_variants():
    assert variant_names()[0] == 'nominal'
    assert load_vehicle(variant='heavy').mass == 3.6
    assert load_vehicle(variant='low_mu').mu == 0.75
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match='unknown variant'):
        load_vehicle(variant='moon')


def test_refuses_an_incomplete_or_untagged_file(tmp_path):
    with open(DEFAULT_FILE) as f:
        data = yaml.safe_load(f)
    del data['vehicle']['mass']
    path = tmp_path / 'v.yaml'
    path.write_text(yaml.safe_dump(data))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match='missing'):
        load_vehicle(path)
    data['vehicle']['mass'] = {'value': 3.5, 'source': 'hunch'}
    path.write_text(yaml.safe_dump(data))
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match='source'):
        load_vehicle(path)


def test_lf_must_lie_inside_the_wheelbase():
    with pytest.raises(ValueError, match='lf'):
        load_vehicle(lf=0.4)
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Implement** — `autosdv_rc_sim/vehicle.yaml`

```yaml
# The RC simulator's vehicle model. Design: docs/design/rc-sim-design.md.
#
# Every value says where it comes from, so the file says how far to trust it
# and what to measure next:
#   measured    on the car
#   config      copied from an AutoSDV file (`from: pkg/path:key`, times `scale`);
#               test_params.py fails if the two drift
#   literature  a published value for a similar car
#   guess       an informed guess
#   mock        a stand-in until someone reads it off the car
#   numerical   a solver setting, not physics
vehicle:
  # --- chassis
  mass:               {value: 3.5,    source: guess,      note: "to be weighed"}
  yaw_inertia:        {value: 0.045,  source: literature, note: "F1TENTH Traxxas: 0.0471 kg m^2"}
  wheel_base:         {value: 0.319,  source: config, from: "autosdv_vehicle_description/config/vehicle_info.param.yaml:wheel_base"}
  lf:                 {value: 0.15,   source: literature, note: "CoM to front axle; lr = wheel_base - lf = 0.169. Measure: balance point"}
  cg_height:          {value: 0.07,   source: literature}
  wheel_radius:       {value: 0.0525, source: config, from: "autosdv_vehicle_interface/params/velocity_report.yaml:wheel_diameter", scale: 0.005, note: "10.5 cm diameter; vehicle_info says 0.052"}
  wheel_axle_inertia: {value: 1.4e-4, source: guess,      note: "per axle: two 50 g wheels, 0.5 m r^2 each"}
  rotor_inertia:      {value: 1.0e-6, source: literature, note: "540-size brushless rotor; reflected x G^2 onto the rear axle"}
  # --- tyres
  mu:                 {value: 0.8,    source: guess,      note: "depends on the floor; tilt or skidpad test (just sim fit-coastdown, fit.skidpad_mu)"}
  pacejka_c:          {value: 1.5,    source: literature, note: "shape: sliding force about 0.7-0.8 of the peak"}
  pacejka_e:          {value: 0.1,    source: literature}
  cornering_stiffness_front: {value: 94.0,  source: literature, note: "F1TENTH, N/rad per axle"}
  cornering_stiffness_rear:  {value: 101.0, source: literature, note: "F1TENTH, N/rad per axle"}
  longitudinal_stiffness_per_load: {value: 15.0, source: guess, note: "C_kappa / Fz"}
  # --- resistances
  rolling_resistance: {value: 0.05,   source: guess,      note: "C_r0; soft RC tyres on a hard floor are 0.03-0.06; refit from a coast-down. Raised from 0.02: it is also the margin that lets every variant follow the 2.5 m/s^2 braking ramp"}
  aero_drag:          {value: 0.0126, source: guess,      note: "0.5 rho Cd A = 0.5 * 1.2 * 0.6 * 0.035 m^2 [N/(m/s)^2]"}
  drivetrain_coulomb: {value: 0.07,   source: guess,      note: "rear axle [N m]; about G Kt I0 with a no-load current of 1.5 A"}
  drivetrain_breakaway: {value: 0.25, source: guess,      note: "rear axle static friction incl. motor cogging [N m]"}
  drivetrain_viscous: {value: 1.0e-4, source: guess,      note: "rear axle [N m s/rad]"}
  front_bearing_coulomb: {value: 0.02,   source: guess,   note: "front axle [N m]; raised from 0.002 with the rolling resistance"}
  # --- motor and battery
  motor_kv:           {value: 3000.0, source: mock,       note: "typical 1/10 brushless; Kt = Ke = 60/(2 pi Kv)"}
  motor_resistance:   {value: 0.03,   source: mock,       note: "winding + ESC [ohm]"}
  gear_ratio:         {value: 14.5,   source: mock,       note: "pinion/spur x differential; no-load top speed 12.6 m/s"}

Refit from car data"}

  v_blend:            {value: 0.05,   source: numerical,  note: "blend width [m/s]"}
  v_min:              {value: 0.15,   source: numerical,  note: "slip denominator floor = v_switch / 2 (CommonRoad)"}
  stick_band:         {value: 2.0,    source: numerical,  note: "Karnopp band [rad/s]: above the largest friction step per dt (0.49 N m / 3.5e-4 * 1 ms = 1.4)"}

# Model variants for grading: coefficients tuned to one exact model lose marks.
# Each must reach 11.4 m/s, launch at 2.5 m/s^2 and brake at 2.5 m/s^2 (test_drivetrain.py), and
# the ESC's full brake must stay under its rear grip: light and low_mu are as light and as slippery
# as the brake torque (sized to the nominal grip) allows, and deadband_wide as wide as the
# resume-from-rest check allows.
variants:
  nominal: {}
  heavy: {mass: 3.6}
  light: {mass: 3.3}
  low_mu: {mu: 0.75}
  high_mu: {mu: 1.0}
  low_batt: {battery_voltage: 10.8}
  kv_low: {motor_kv: 2850.0}
  deadband_wide: {esc_deadband: 7}
```

`autosdv_rc_sim/params.py`:

```python
"""The RC simulator's vehicle parameters: one YAML file, every value tagged with its source.

See vehicle.yaml for the format and the meaning of the tags.
"""
from dataclasses import dataclass, fields
import math
from pathlib import Path
from typing import Any, Dict, List

import yaml

GRAVITY = 9.81
SOURCES = ('measured', 'config', 'literature', 'guess', 'mock', 'numerical')
DEFAULT_FILE = Path(__file__).resolve().parent / 'vehicle.yaml'


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class VehicleParams:
    """The model's parameters (SI units, PWM in PCA9685 counts); see vehicle.yaml."""

    mass: float
    yaw_inertia: float
    wheel_base: float
    lf: float
    cg_height: float
    wheel_radius: float
    wheel_axle_inertia: float
    rotor_inertia: float
    mu: float
    pacejka_c: float
    pacejka_e: float
    cornering_stiffness_front: float
    cornering_stiffness_rear: float
    longitudinal_stiffness_per_load: float
    rolling_resistance: float
    aero_drag: float
    drivetrain_coulomb: float
    drivetrain_breakaway: float
    drivetrain_viscous: float
    front_bearing_coulomb: float
    motor_kv: float
    motor_resistance: float
    gear_ratio: float
    battery_voltage: float
    battery_resistance: float
    pwm_freq: float
    init_pwm: int
    min_pwm: int
    max_pwm: int
    brake_pwm: int
    esc_deadband: int
    throttle_ramp: float
    drag_brake: float
    brake_torque_max: float
    esc_current_limit: float
    esc_regen_current_limit: float
    reverse_scale: float
    rearm_time: float
    init_steer: int
    min_steer: int
    max_steer: int
    tire_angle_to_steer_ratio: float
    max_steer_angle: float
    servo_dead_time: float
    servo_rate_limit: float
    markers_per_rotation: int
    gyro_noise_std: float
    accel_noise_std: float
    gyro_bias_z: float
    physics_dt: float
    v_switch: float
    v_blend: float
    v_min: float
    stick_band: float

    @property
    def lr(self) -> float:
        return self.wheel_base - self.lf

    @property
    def kt(self) -> float:
        """Torque constant [N m/A] = back-EMF constant [V s/rad] = 60 / (2 pi Kv)."""
        return 60.0 / (2.0 * math.pi * self.motor_kv)

    @property
    def ke(self) -> float:
        return self.kt

    @property
    def front_axle_inertia(self) -> float:
        return self.wheel_axle_inertia

    @property
    def rear_axle_inertia(self) -> float:
        return self.wheel_axle_inertia + self.rotor_inertia * self.gear_ratio * self.gear_ratio

    @property
    def effective_mass(self) -> float:
        """Mass plus both axles' rotational inertia, referred to the ground."""
        r2 = self.wheel_radius * self.wheel_radius
        return self.mass + (self.front_axle_inertia + self.rear_axle_inertia) / r2

    @property
    def fz_front_static(self) -> float:
        return self.mass * GRAVITY * self.lr / self.wheel_base

    @property
    def fz_rear_static(self) -> float:
        return self.mass * GRAVITY * self.lf / self.wheel_base

    @property
    def b_lat_front(self) -> float:
        """Pacejka B, front lateral: C*B*mu*Fz equals the cornering stiffness at static load."""
        return self.cornering_stiffness_front / (self.pacejka_c * self.mu * self.fz_front_static)

    @property
    def b_lat_rear(self) -> float:
        return self.cornering_stiffness_rear / (self.pacejka_c * self.mu * self.fz_rear_static)

    @property
    def b_long(self) -> float:
        return self.longitudinal_stiffness_per_load / (self.pacejka_c * self.mu)


_INT_FIELDS = {f.name for f in fields(VehicleParams) if f.type is int}
_NAMES = [f.name for f in fields(VehicleParams)]


@dataclass
class TaggedFile:
    entries: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]
    variants: Dict[str, Dict[str, Any]]

    def values(self) -> Dict[str, Any]:
        return {key: entry['value'] for key, entry in self.entries.items()}


def load_tagged(path=None) -> TaggedFile:
    """Read and check the tagged file: every field once, every tag known."""
    path = Path(path) if path else DEFAULT_FILE
    with open(path) as f:
        data = yaml.safe_load(f) or {}
    entries = data.get('vehicle') or {}
    variants = data.get('variants') or {}
    missing = [n for n in _NAMES if n not in entries]
    unknown = [k for k in entries if k not in _NAMES]
    if missing or unknown:
        raise ValueError(f'{path}: missing {missing}, unknown {unknown}')
    for key, entry in entries.items():
        if not isinstance(entry, dict) or 'value' not in entry or 'source' not in entry:
            raise ValueError(f'{path}: {key} must be {{value: ..., source: ...}}')
        if entry['source'] not in SOURCES:
            raise ValueError(f"{path}: {key}: source {entry['source']!r} is not one of {SOURCES}")
        if entry['source'] == 'config' and 'from' not in entry:
            raise ValueError(f'{path}: {key} is tagged config but names no file (from: pkg/path:key)')
    if 'nominal' not in variants:
        raise ValueError(f'{path}: variants must include nominal')
    for name, over in variants.items():
        bad = [k for k in (over or {}) if k not in _NAMES]
        if bad:
            raise ValueError(f'{path}: variant {name} sets unknown {bad}')
    return TaggedFile(entries, {k: dict(v or {}) for k, v in variants.items()})


def _coerce(name: str, value):
    if name in _INT_FIELDS:
        if isinstance(value, bool) or int(value) != value:
            raise ValueError(f'{name} must be an integer, got {value!r}')
        return int(value)
    return float(value)


def validate(p: VehicleParams) -> None:
    """Refuse parameter sets the model cannot run."""
    if not 0.0 < p.lf < p.wheel_base:
        raise ValueError(f'lf ({p.lf}) must lie inside the wheelbase ({p.wheel_base})')
    if p.pacejka_c < 1.0 or not 0.0 <= p.pacejka_e < 1.0:
        raise ValueError('need pacejka_c >= 1 (the peak is reached) and 0 <= pacejka_e < 1')
    if not p.min_pwm < p.init_pwm - p.esc_deadband < p.init_pwm + p.esc_deadband < p.max_pwm:
        raise ValueError('the PWM range must extend beyond the ESC deadband on both sides')
    for name in ('mass', 'yaw_inertia', 'wheel_radius', 'wheel_axle_inertia', 'mu', 'motor_kv',
                 'motor_resistance', 'gear_ratio', 'battery_voltage', 'pwm_freq', 'physics_dt',
                 'esc_current_limit', 'esc_regen_current_limit',
                 'v_min', 'v_blend', 'stick_band', 'servo_rate_limit'):
        if getattr(p, name) <= 0.0:
            raise ValueError(f'{name} must be positive')


def load_vehicle(path=None, variant: str = 'nominal', **overrides) -> VehicleParams:
    """The parameters of one variant, with optional per-field overrides (tests)."""
    tagged = load_tagged(path)
    if variant not in tagged.variants:
        raise ValueError(f'unknown variant {variant!r}; known: {sorted(tagged.variants)}')
    values = tagged.values()
    values.update(tagged.variants[variant])
    values.update(overrides)
    p = VehicleParams(**{k: _coerce(k, v) for k, v in values.items()})
    validate(p)
    return p


def variant_names(path=None) -> List[str]:
    return list(load_tagged(path).variants)
```

## Task 6: Deterministic transcendental functions `detmath.py`

**Files:**
- Create: `src/sim/autosdv_rc_sim/autosdv_rc_sim/detmath.py`
- Create: `src/sim/autosdv_rc_sim/test/test_detmath.py`

**Interfaces:**
- Produces: `PI`, `HALF_PI`, `sqrt` (= `math.sqrt`), `sin(x)`, `cos(x)`, `atan(x)`, `atan2(y, x)`, `exp(x)`, `tanh(x)`. All are float → float, built only from IEEE basic operations, `round`, `math.ldexp` and `math.sqrt`.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing test** — `test/test_detmath.py`

```python
"""detmath agrees with libm to a few ulp; its value comes from basic arithmetic alone."""
import math

import pytest

from autosdv_rc_sim import detmath as dm


def grid(lo, hi, n):
    return [lo + (hi - lo) * i / (n - 1) for i in range(n)]


@pytest.mark.parametrize('f, ref, xs', [
    (dm.sin, math.sin, grid(-20.0, 20.0, 4001)),
    (dm.cos, math.cos, grid(-20.0, 20.0, 4001)),
    (dm.atan, math.atan, grid(-50.0, 50.0, 4001) + [1e-300, 1e300, -1e300]),
    (dm.exp, math.exp, grid(-30.0, 30.0, 4001)),
    (dm.tanh, math.tanh, grid(-25.0, 25.0, 4001) + [1e-8, -3e-6]),
])
def test_close_to_libm(f, ref, xs):
    for x in xs:
        assert f(x) == pytest.approx(ref(x), rel=4e-15, abs=4e-16), x


def test_atan2_quadrants():
    for y in (-2.0, -0.5, 0.0, 0.5, 2.0):
        for x in (-3.0, -1.0, 1.0, 3.0):
            assert dm.atan2(y, x) == pytest.approx(math.atan2(y, x), rel=4e-15, abs=4e-16)
    assert dm.atan2(1.0, 0.0) == dm.HALF_PI
    assert dm.atan2(-1.0, 0.0) == -dm.HALF_PI
    assert dm.atan2(0.0, 0.0) == 0.0


def test_special_values():
    assert dm.exp(800.0) == math.inf
    assert dm.exp(-800.0) == 0.0
    assert dm.tanh(30.0) == 1.0 and dm.tanh(-30.0) == -1.0
    assert math.isnan(dm.atan(float('nan')))
```
